section Generic

variable {O : Type*} [CommRing O] [IsDomain O] [IsDiscreteValuationRing O] {π : O}

/-- ★ **The μ₃ (two-level) analogue of C.34's `dvSideSet_nonempty`.** Transcribed verbatim
from that proof's mechanism, one level up: a nonzero `f` has a nonzero `Ψ`-development digit
(else `f` would reconstruct to `0`), that digit has finite level-2 height (`dv2Pin`'s unfold to
`dvSupp F (dev Ψ f j) L.u L.ℓ`, finite by `C130s6.dvSupp_ne_top_of_ne_zero` — no degree
restriction), the finite-range infimum defining `dv2Supp` is attained, and `0 < ℓ₂` excludes the
`⊤`-height abscissa (`ℓ₂ • ⊤ = ⊤`) from being the attaining one. -/
theorem dv2SideSet_nonempty {F : KeyFrame O π} {H₀ hpin} (L : LevelDatum F H₀ hpin)
    {Ψ : Polynomial O} (hΨ : Ψ.Monic) (hΨd : 0 < Ψ.natDegree) {f : Polynomial O} (hf : f ≠ 0)
    (u₂ : ℕ) {ℓ₂ : ℕ} (hℓ₂ : 0 < ℓ₂) : (dv2SideSet L Ψ f u₂ ℓ₂).Nonempty := by
  classical
  -- a nonzero `Ψ`-development digit exists within the support range
  have hblock : ∃ j ∈ Finset.range (f.natDegree + 1), dev Ψ f j ≠ 0 := by
    by_contra hall
    push Not at hall
    refine hf ?_
    have hrep : ∑ j ∈ Finset.range (f.natDegree + 1), dev Ψ f j * Ψ ^ j = f :=
      sum_dev_eq hΨ hΨd f
        (Nat.lt_of_lt_of_le (Nat.lt_succ_self _) (Nat.le_mul_of_pos_right _ hΨd))
    rw [← hrep]
    exact Finset.sum_eq_zero fun j hj => by rw [hall j hj, zero_mul]
  obtain ⟨j₀, hj₀mem, hj₀⟩ := hblock
  have hhgt₀ : dv2Pin L Ψ f j₀ ≠ ⊤ := by
    rw [dv2Pin, dv2Hgt]
    exact dvSupp_ne_top_of_ne_zero F L.u L.ℓ hj₀
  -- hence the cleared level-2 support is finite
  have hfin : dv2Supp L Ψ f u₂ ℓ₂ ≠ ⊤ := by
    obtain ⟨m, hm⟩ := WithTop.ne_top_iff_exists.mp hhgt₀
    have hle : dv2Supp L Ψ f u₂ ℓ₂ ≤ ((ℓ₂ * m + u₂ * j₀ : ℕ) : ℕ∞) := by
      simp only [dv2Supp]
      refine le_trans (Finset.inf_le hj₀mem) ?_
      rw [← hm, nsmul_eq_mul]

codex
Overall verdict: the semantic finding survives. Claims 1, 2, and 5 are correct. Claims 3 and 4 identify genuine failure mechanisms under a pinned/power-basis reading, but each overstates what follows for the exact opaque `Classical.choose` body. Those are GAPs, not CRITICAL errors.

## Claim 1 — CONFIRM

> “For every r < f₁, t < f₂: Δ(r,t) := e₁e₂h·r + e₂u₂·t satisfies Δ(r,t) < E₂ < lam…”

Indeed,

\[
e_1e_2hr < e_1e_2hf_1=e_2D'h<u_2
\]

by `r < f₁` and `hfloor`. Also,

\[
e_2u_2t\le e_2u_2(f_2-1).
\]

Since `0 < e₂`,

\[
\Delta(r,t)<u_2+e_2u_2(f_2-1)\le e_2u_2f_2=E_2.
\]

Thus natural subtraction does not truncate, and since `e₂ ∣ Δ(r,t)`,

\[
(\lambda-\Delta(r,t))\bmod e_2=\lambda\bmod e_2.
\]

The predicate searched by `towerSolve` depends only on `k % e₂`, so `b₀` is constant over `(r,t)`. Coprimality makes multiplication by `u₂` a permutation modulo `e₂`; consequently the solution is unique, lies below `e₂`, and is zero exactly when `lam % e₂ = 0`.

No error.

## Claim 2 — CONFIRM

> “Each (r,t) term … is C(c)·X^(i+e₁r)·F.key^(b₀+e₂t) with i + e₁r < D′…”

From `slotIdx_spec`, `i < e₁`; hence for `r < f₁`,

\[
i+e_1r<e_1+e_1(f_1-1)=e_1f_1=D'.
\]

Therefore the coefficient multiplying each power of `F.key` is already reduced modulo the monic key. Distinct `r` give disjoint exponent intervals

\[
[e_1r,e_1r+e_1-1],
\]

so there is no cancellation between different `r`. Distinct `t` give distinct exponents `b₀+e₂t`. Consequently the asserted digit-support formula is correct.

For fixed `t`, the digit vanishes iff every coefficient

\[
\operatorname{resLift}(\operatorname{stageCoord}(k2Coord(s,t),r))\pi^{a_0}
\]

vanishes. Domainhood, `π ≠ 0`, and nonvanishing of `resLift` on nonzero residues reduce this to all stage coordinates being zero, hence `k2Coord T s t = 0`.

No error. Notice that this conclusion concerns the chosen polynomial’s coefficient; it does not say that these coefficients faithfully recover `s`.

## Claim 3 — CONFIRM the mechanism; GAP in the unqualified non-provability inference

> “If e₂ ∤ lam then b₀ ≠ 0 and each abscissa b₀ + e₂t … is … NOT divisible by e₂.”

Correct. Since `b₀ < e₂` and `b₀ ≠ 0`,

\[
b_0+e_2t<e_2+e_2(f_2-1)=e_2f_2
\]

and its residue modulo `e₂` is `b₀`.

> “IsTestKey's clause 4 then demands the lift's digit to vanish at ALL these abscissae, i.e. k2Coord T s t = 0 for ALL t < f₂.”

Correct, using Claim 2 and `dev_sub`.

> “Hence the axiom is not provable…”

This inference has a GAP. The support calculation proves that the conclusion entails low-coefficient vanishing for every *eligible* `s`; it does not alone establish eligibility or formal non-derivability. Moreover, the exact body uses an opaque choice witness.

The finding nevertheless survives under a pinned/power-basis interpretation. Eligibility is not merely speculative: because the signed statement permits `μ₂ = 0`, take `f = 1`. Then:

- `1 ∈ towerLocus T 0`;
- the level-2 side is the singleton `{0}` for every `lam`;
- its residual polynomial is `1`;
- `(X - C s)^0 = 1`.

Thus every nonzero `s` is eligible at every `lam > E₂`. For the landed `s2Tower`, `e₂ = 2` and `E₂ = 10`, so `lam = 11` is an off-lattice eligible height. Under canonical coordinates, taking `s = 1` gives a nonzero low coordinate, and clause 4 fails.

So this is a justification GAP, not a CRITICAL error.

## Claim 4 — CONFIRM the mechanism; GAP in treating the opaque body as already pinned

> “At a slot t with (towerLabel T).coeff t = 0, the composed key's digit is 0, so the corrected key's digit is −(lift digit)…”

Correct. The landed theorem `composedKey_zero_slot` establishes the first equality, and Claim 2 gives nonvanishing exactly when `k2Coord T s t ≠ 0`.

> “s ≠ 0 and hres do not constrain the SUPPORT of s to lie inside the support of towerLabel T.”

Correct, decisively so because `μ₂ = 0` makes `hres` equal `1 = 1` independently of `s`.

The `X²+1` over `F₃` example correctly shows that a legal label can have an interior zero coefficient. With `e₂ = 1`, clause 4 is vacuous and every `lam` is divisible by `e₂`. Under canonical coordinates, take `s` with a nonzero `β`-coordinate at the missing `t = 1` slot; clause 5’s zero branch fails.

The GAP is the same opacity qualification as in Claim 3: the legality note establishes the label shape, while the actual failure additionally uses a faithful coordinate selector. The exact opaque selector is not definitionally known to be that selector.

No CRITICAL error.

## Claim 5 — CONFIRM

> “The source clause EFF.GENTOW1.28 claims … ‘level-1 development one-sided … with residual psi2 and side digits byte-unchanged …’”

That is the exact source package in [EFF-GENTOW1.md](/data/users/asvin/math-and-lean/p-adic-splitting-densities/spec/EFF-GENTOW1.md:879).

> “It does NOT assert that Φ₂⁺ lies in the rigid test family with EXACT-ZERO off-lattice and zero-slot digits.”

Correct. “Side digits byte-unchanged” concerns the digits attaining the old side and their graded residual data. A new nonzero term strictly above that side does not alter those invariants, but it is still literally a nonzero development digit. Thus perturbation-stability does not imply exact-zero support.

> “Does ‘keeps every T(b)' key invariant … hence the (i)-forcing chain reruns’ license the IsTestKey transcription?”

No. `IsTestKey` was a sufficient carrier used to prove the forcing package for the original key. It does not follow that every polynomial preserving the resulting side, residual, and carrier invariants must again satisfy the syntactically stronger exact-zero predicate. That would reverse a sufficient implication without an equivalence theorem.

Therefore the leanspec conclusion overclaims relative to the cited source.

## Escape hatches

### (a) Can `hne + hres + hslope` force `e₂ ∣ lam`?

No. There is no denominator/parity obstruction: the outer side is explicitly read with denominator `1`, so `lam` is simply an integer slope numerator.

More strongly, `μ₂ = 0`, `f = 1` makes the hypotheses hold for arbitrary `lam > E₂` and arbitrary nonzero `s`. Taking `s2Tower`, where `e₂ = 2`, `E₂ = 10`, and `lam = 11`, gives an eligible off-lattice height.

### (b) Can `hres` force support of `s` inside support of `towerLabel T`?

No. At `μ₂ = 0`,

\[
(X-C(s))^{\mu_2}=1
\]

for every `s`, so `hres` contains no information about `s` whatsoever. Even for positive `μ₂`, the equality can identify an element `s`; it does not imply that its power-basis support is contained in the coefficient support of the defining polynomial.

### (c) Could all hypotheses be vacuous, and is that provable?

No. The zero-multiplicity branch supplies a direct mathematical witness:

- `f = 1`;
- `μ₂ = 0`;
- any `lam > E₂`;
- any `s ≠ 0`.

The necessary unit facts are present in the corpus: `isDvPure_one` and `dvResPoly_one` are landed in [C133mh9.lean](/data/users/asvin/math-and-lean/p-adic-splitting-densities/leanfinal/Uniformity/ChapC/C133mh9.lean:1279), and generic level-2 side nonemptiness is landed in [C132nv6.lean](/data/users/asvin/math-and-lean/p-adic-splitting-densities/leanfinal/Uniformity/ChapC/C132nv6.lean:101). Thus universal vacuity is not a viable escape.

### (d) Other escape: the pathological opaque-choice reading

Yes—this is the one genuine reading under which the signed conclusion can hold.

Because `(towerLabel T).coeff 0 ≠ 0`, the class `β = AdjoinRoot.root` is a unit. Hence every `s` has a representative divisible by `X^f₂`:

\[
s=\beta^{f_2}q(\beta)
\]

for a suitable `q`. Such a representative has all coefficients below `f₂` equal to zero. A choice function that always selects representatives of this form makes

\[
k2Coord(T,s,t)=0\qquad(t<f_2)
\]

for every `s`. Then `k2DigitLift` is identically zero, and the conclusion reduces to the already-landed `IsTestKey` theorem for `composedKey`.

This is a legitimate model-theoretic realization of the underspecified choice, but it is not a property Lean can derive from `AdjoinRoot.mk_surjective(...).choose`. Conversely, a canonical remainder/power-basis selector produces the counterexamples above.

Therefore:

- the exact opaque term is not uniformly semantically false under every possible realization of choice;
- the intended/pinned coordinate reading is false;
- and the source-to-leanspec transcription still overclaims.

- and the source-to-leanspec transcription still overclaims.
